Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

Hard-block primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_9x9`   (precision INT9)
  computes: acc += a*b
  input ports:  clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  output ports: out[18]
  arithmetic: the block's operands and result are unsigned. A design operand narrower than its port must be zero-extended to the port width. A signed design operand has no exact representation on this block. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  timing: `out` is registered inside the block on `clk`; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
  registers: the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
  reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low; it is the only control of the running sum (no enable, no load), and every register is unknown until that first clear.
  power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  Wire EVERY operand of the fused region onto the input ports (name them exactly as above); drive the region's real consumer from the output port. Tie unused term ports to 0. Do not leave input ports unconnected.

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a critical path of 6.06654 ns and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132, four blocks per tile in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode. Its used logic-block area is 5.69025e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 3.45201e+08. Its slowest stage is in output_logic: no multiply, the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 1.50 ns from the pad into the first LUT, 1.91 into the third. The next slowest stage, in output_logic, is no multiply, a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT, 6.008 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Initial values. A rewrite may not rely on any register's initial value, soft or hard: the flow drops initial values, so every register starts at an unknown value and holds one until something writes it.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone. Keep every term of the original expression, including ports every instantiation ties to a constant. A term that goes on no block port (an addend beside the products) stays outside the block, added to its result; the flow flattens the design and removes an add of a constant, so such a term puts nothing on the stage and rule 2 does not count it. A bound on a port's values may be used only if the plan states it and names its source in the design, on a line of its own beginning ASSUMES:; such a rewrite is recorded as verified under that assumption. Fold a term only when it is constant inside the module itself, and say so in the plan.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A DSP tile is in one mode, and in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode one tile holds four blocks; the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock, so blocks with different reset signals cannot share a tile and each takes a tile of its own. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Measured on this architecture

Block delay, clock-to-Q and blocks per tile are from the arch file; wire and adder delays are sampled, a range with the median in parentheses.

Every integer DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile when they share one reset and one clock (the tile has one of each); blocks on different resets take a tile each.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

This mode's block holds registers

The rules above were written for blocks with no registers. This run's target mode is a registered block: its contract states a latency in cycles, where its registers sit, what its reset does and which ports are tied off. Read the rules above with the changes below.

Must hold

Register control, for this mode. The block has two register stages of its own: one on the operands, before the products, and one on the sum. A design register may go inside the block only as one of those two stages. A register on the operands, or on each product before the sum, is the first stage; the register that holds the sum is the second. The block's registers have the reset the contract states, no enable and no initial value: every one is unknown until the block's reset clears it, and the contract's timing line says when that clear lands. A design register that has an enable, a different reset behaviour, or a reset signal other than the one you connect to the block's reset port cannot be replaced by the block's register: keep it outside or leave the site alone. Connect the block's reset to the signal that reset the registers it replaces. If those registers had no reset and the block must start from a defined state (a running sum, whose value is unknown until the first clear), drive the block's reset from a reset the design asserts only at start-up, and state that assumption in the plan on an ASSUMES: line (the rewrite is then verified under it); if they had no reset and the block needs no defined start (a result that depends on the last operands alone), tie the block's reset to 0.

Latency (rule 5, and it must hold). The module's latency must be unchanged: every output carries the same value on the same cycle as before. Count the register stages the original has from the operands to the register that holds the sum, and compare with the block's latency.

the original hasthen
as many stages as the blockthe block's registers stand in place of the design's; remove exactly those and move nothing else
morethe extra stage stays outside the block, on the operands before it or on the result after it, where it was
fewerthe block adds the difference: move a register that already exists on the same path into the block's place, or delay every other value that meets the block's result by the same number of cycles so that the stage adds values from the same cycle; if neither can be done without changing when the module's outputs change, leave the site alone

State in the plan the stages the original has around each site, which of them the block replaces, and the module's latency before and after. They must be equal.

Addends and the chain input. A term the original adds to the products before the sum's register cannot be added to the block's result on the same cycle: the result is already registered. The chain input is a dedicated pin that takes only another block's chain output or a constant. If the term is a module port that every instantiation ties to a constant, connect that port to the chain input: the block is then tied off exactly where the original was, and the module still computes what it computed for every value of its ports. Any other addend needs its own register (same reset) and an add after the block, which rule 2 counts on the next stage.

Weigh

Stages (rules 2 and 3). A registered block ends the stage that reaches its operand ports and starts a new one at its result. The wire into the block and the wire out of it are on different stages. Soft arithmetic after the block is on the stage after the block's result register, not in series with the block's delay. Between its own two registers the block takes its fixed delay from the table.

Flip-flops (rule 4). The design registers the block replaces leave the soft logic.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

Read the source. Output EXACTLY this header (uppercase keys), then the plan:

TIER: T1 | T2 | NONE
REGIONS: <comma-separated region id(s)> # one id for a direct swap; N ids for a fusion
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal/module the terms feed> # or NONE for a single region
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, a single integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, a single integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage your rewrite leaves, in ns, a single number>
MAPPING: <which operands map to which of the mode's ports>
PLAN: <how the module's live inputs/outputs and downstream consumers are preserved; any introduced
operations/datatypes (should be NONE for T1/T2); and the main risks>

Output rules: no preamble before the TIER line — read the source first, then write. You
are one step of an automated pipeline: a reviewer runs automatically on this plan, you
cannot invoke it, and no human reads your output, so do not end with a question or an
offer.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.v

response

I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: out_temp in seq_mac (the running accumulator written at tpu_like.small.os.v:4699-4701, fed by qadd add_u1 at :4697) — one accumulator per seq_mac, 256 instances
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 6.06654
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — a[9] = {1'b0, a_flopped[7:0]} (:4677, :4684), b[9] = {1'b0, b_flopped[7:0]} (:4678, :4685), clk = clk, reset = reset (the existing seq_mac port, driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110), out[17:0] → out_temp is replaced by out[15:0], which drives the existing saturating downcast at :4704-4716 and so out_c/matrixC*. No other input port exists in this mode; no term port is left unconnected and none needs a constant tie beyond the zero-extension bit on a and b.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110; pe_reset is the self-clearing REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR[15] bit, cfg:4868/4883) is asserted only at start-up / between matmul runs and never during an accumulation whose result is read. The baseline out_temp has no reset and no initial value, so its start value is undefined; the block's running sum is defined only after its first clear. Under this assumption the block is bit-exact with the baseline from the first post-reset edge onward.

PLAN:

What is converted. seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666-4718), instantiated once per PE in all three PE variants (processing_element:4455, processing_element_top_edge:4551, processing_element_bottom_edge:4551) — 256 instances, all on the same clk and the same effective_rst. Inside each, the fused region is exactly qmult mult_u1 (:4689, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier), the register mul_out_temp_reg (:4691-4693), qadd add_u1 (:4697, c = a + b) and the accumulator register out_temp (:4699-4701). These four are deleted and replaced by one mac_int_9x9. a_flopped/b_flopped (:4683-4686) stay in soft logic, outside the block, feeding its a/b ports.

Arithmetic is exact. DWIDTH is 8 (:74). qmult's operands are plain [7:0] vectors, so the multiply is unsigned — matching the block's unsigned ports — and zero-extension 8→9 is the allowed case in the table. The full product 255×255 = 65025 fits in 16 bits, so no product bit is lost on the way into the 18-bit sum. The baseline accumulator is 16 bits and wraps mod 2^16; the block wraps mod 2^18, and since carries propagate low→high, out[15:0] of the block equals the baseline out_temp bit for bit — the "original's narrow sum register wraps / the low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrap" row. Nothing is truncated before the sum in the baseline, so the forbidden truncate-before-sum case does not arise. The saturating downcast at :4704-4716 reinterprets out_temp[15] as a sign bit; that logic is untouched soft logic reading out[15:0], so its behaviour is unchanged.

Observability. out_temp is read by exactly two things: qadd (the accumulation itself, which goes inside the block) and the downcast at :4704-4716. The downcast is driven from the block's visible out[15:0]. mul_out_temp_reg and the qmult result are read by nothing else (qmult/qadd are leaf modules with a single consumer each). a/b are operands and are exempt; in_a/in_b continue to drive the PE's out_a/out_b shift registers unchanged. out_c → matrixC* → output_logic (:2331-2346) is unchanged.

Registers and latency. Baseline seq_mac has three register stages from a/b to the accumulator: a_flopped/b_flopped (edge k), mul_out_temp_reg (edge k+1), out_temp (edge k+2) — a product applied before edge k lands in out_temp after edge k+2. The block supplies two stages (operand register, then sum register; operands before edge k appear in out after edge k+1). The original has one stage more, so the extra stage stays outside, on the operands where it already was: a_flopped/b_flopped are kept and drive the block's ports. Then a value applied before edge k is in a_flopped at edge k, in the block's operand registers at edge k+1, and in the block's sum after edge k+2 — identical to baseline. Module latency before = 3 edges, after = 3 edges; every seq_mac output changes on the same cycle as before. The block replaces mul_out_temp_reg (by its operand register — the multiply is purely combinational between the two, so registering the operands instead of the product gives the same values on the same cycles) and out_temp (by its sum register). Neither baseline register has an enable, a load, or a reset, matching the block's contract: the block adds one product on every edge with no enable, exactly as qadd+out_temp did. The only register-control difference is that the block's registers are cleared by reset, which is the ASSUMES: line above; a_flopped/b_flopped remain unreset, as in the baseline, and during a reset window the PEs' in_a/in_b are zero anyway (PE out_a/out_b are reset at :4457-4466), so the first post-reset products agree.

Delay (rule 1). The design's slowest stage is in output_logic (reset pad → four LUTs → FF, 6.06654 ns) and the next is 6.008 ns, also output_logic; neither contains a multiply and this rewrite does not touch either, so the clock period does not improve — the gain here is area, and that is stated plainly. The stages the rewrite creates: (1) a_flopped/b_flopped → block operand ports: clock-to-Q 0.06 + one register-to-first-block wire, 1.58 ns at the median and 3.25 ns at the worst sampled wire; (2) inside the block, its fixed 2.14 ns; (3) block out → the downcast LUTs → output_logic's c_data_out* FFs: 0.06 + one block-into-soft-logic wire (0.96 median, 1.90 worst) + the same downcast and output-mux LUTs the baseline already had on that stage. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one — the block is registered, so its input wire and output wire are on different stages and no two blocks are ever in series. Soft logic that leaves a stage: the 16-bit qadd ripple adder and its routing, removed from stage (3)'s source side (it was between out_temp and itself, i.e. on the accumulator's own feedback stage, which this rewrite eliminates entirely).

Risk on stage (3). Stage (3) is the one stage whose delay could rise: its source register moves from a cluster into a DSP tile, so a cluster-output wire is traded for a block-into-soft-logic wire (0.54–1.90, median 0.96). If that path is the 6.008 ns stage, a worst-case wire could push it past 6.06654 and make it the new critical path. Working the other way, the rewrite removes roughly 8000 BLEs (see below), which shrinks the placement and should shorten output_logic's own long wires (2.64 ns into the last LUT today); I do not claim that improvement, I report the period as unchanged at 6.06654 ns.

Area. 256 blocks, all sharing one clock and one reset, pack four to a tile = 64 tiles. The 32 multiplies that remain (16 in norm, 16 in activation) are all 8×8; at the 2-per-tile density the baseline already shows for its non-quad tiles they take 16 tiles, giving 80 tiles total — 8 fewer than the baseline's 88 and well inside the device's 132. If the packer reaches 4-per-tile on those leftovers the total is 72; I do not assume it. Per PE the rewrite deletes a 16-bit adder (~16 LUTs) plus 32 flip-flops (mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp); across 256 PEs that is ~8192 BLEs, close to 800 of the ~1239 clusters the baseline uses (5.69025e7 − 88×253779 = 3.457e7, /27905 ≈ 1239). Taking a deliberately pessimistic saving of 600 clusters: area ≈ 80×253779 + 639×27905 ≈ 3.81e7, and area-delay ≈ 3.81e7 × 6.06654 ≈ 2.31e8 against the baseline 3.45201e8 — a clear improvement even before counting the tile reduction optimistically. Rule 4's caution applies: the flip-flops are the certain saving, the adder LUTs are not counted on.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. No new arithmetic, no width or signedness change, no new control signal — reset and clk are already ports of seq_mac.

Candidates left alone.

  • norm.$mul$...:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 isolated multiplies, norm:5067): each product feeds only its own element of variance_applied_data and reaches no sum at all. An isolated scalar multiply does not fit a sum-of-products/MAC mode; the block would impose a running accumulation the design does not compute. Also each is truncated to 8 bits into a register whose control is reset || ~enable_norm plus the in_data_available || norm_in_progress enable and the validity_mask[i] mux (:5047-5072) — a register with an enable and a different reset, which the block's registers cannot stand in for.
  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (sum_in=top) (the 4 whose products are later summed): the sum they feed is the pooling add in pool (:5389, four bytes added then >> 2), reached only after each product has been truncated to 8 bits and latched in variance_applied_data (:5067), published on norm.out_data through the enable_norm bypass mux (:5029) and carried across module boundaries. Three independent blockers: products truncated before the sum (explicitly disallowed — fusing and truncating after differs); each product is read by a module output port and by downstream consumers other than the sum (observability); and the pool sum is a one-shot add of four different elements with a shift, inside a register with its own reset/enable (:5364-5392), not a running accumulation that adds one product on every edge, which is the only thing mac_int_9x9 can compute.
  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 sites, activation:5479): each is data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i], truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal (:5479), and the add at :5482 happens a cycle later against data_intercept_delayed, a different value every cycle. That is not a running sum: the mode has no enable and no load and adds one product on every edge, so it cannot reproduce a one-shot product + intercept latched into a distinct register. Each product also feeds a separate output element, each is only one product in its sum, the product is truncated before the add, and both registers carry the reset reset || ~enable_activation with the in_data_available || activation_in_progress enable (:5463-5486) — a register control the block's registers do not have.

5004	module norm(
5005	    input enable_norm,
5006	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean,
5007	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inv_var,
5008	    input in_data_available,
5009	    input [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
5010	    output [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
5011	    output out_data_available,
5012	    input [`MASK_WIDTH-1:0] validity_mask,
5013	    output done_norm,
5014	    input clk,
5015	    input reset
5016	);
5017	
5018	reg out_data_available_internal;
5019	wire [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
5020	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_data;
5021	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data;
5022	reg done_norm_internal;
5023	reg norm_in_progress;
5024	reg in_data_available_flopped;
5025	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data_flopped;
5026	
5027	//Muxing logic to handle the case when this block is disabled
5028	assign out_data_available = (enable_norm) ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available_flopped;
5029	assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data_flopped;
5030	assign done_norm = (enable_norm) ? done_norm_internal : 1'b1;
5031	
5032	//inp_data will have multiple elements in it. the number of elements is the same as size of the matmul.
5033	//on each clock edge, if in_data_available is 1, then we will normalize the inputs.
5034	
5035	//the code uses the funky part-select syntax. example:
5036	//wire [7:0] byteN = word[byte_num*8 +: 8];
5037	//byte_num*8 is the starting point. 8 is the width is the part-select (has to be constant).in_data_available
5038	//+: indicates the part-select increases from the starting point
5039	//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point
5040	//another example:
5041	//loc = 3;
5042	//PA[loc -:4] = PA[loc+1 +:4];  // equivalent to PA[3:0] = PA[7:4];
5043	
5044	reg [31:0] cycle_count;
5045	reg [31:0] i;
5046	always @(posedge clk) begin
5047	    if ((reset || ~enable_norm)) begin
5048	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5049	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5050	        out_data_available_internal <= 0;
5051	        cycle_count <= 0;
5052	        done_norm_internal <= 0;
5053	        norm_in_progress <= 0;
5054	        in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available;
5055	        inp_data_flopped <= inp_data;
5056	    end else if (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) begin
5057	        cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
5058	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5059	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5060	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5061	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
5062	        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
5063	        //this module in 1 clock.
5064	        for (i = 0; i < `DESIGN_SIZE; i=i+1) begin
5065	            if (validity_mask[i] == 1'b1) begin
5066	                mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean);
5067	                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] * inv_var);
5068	            end 
5069	            else begin
5070	                mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
5071	                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
5072	            end
5073	        end
5074	
5075	        //Out data is available starting with the second clock cycle because 
5076	        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.
5077	        if(cycle_count==2) begin
5078	            out_data_available_internal <= 1;
5079	        end
5080	
5081	        //When we've normalized values N times, where N is the matmul
5082	        //size, that means we're done. But there is one additional cycle
5083	        //that is taken in the beginning (when we are applying the mean to the first
5084	        //column of data). We can call thi

Target mode: mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode Tier: T2 Rule: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.
Regions the plan selects (1): qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
(op 'mac', datatype int8, consumers ['qmult']).
Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a critical path of 6.06654 ns and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132, four blocks per tile in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode. Its used logic-block area is 5.69025e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 3.45201e+08. Its slowest stage is in output_logic: no multiply, the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 1.50 ns from the pad into the first LUT, 1.91 into the third. The next slowest stage, in output_logic, is no multiply, a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT, 6.008 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.
The block's contract, its lines on timing, registers and tie-offs:
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).

--- PLAN ---
I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: out_temp in seq_mac (the running accumulator written at tpu_like.small.os.v:4699-4701, fed by qadd add_u1 at :4697) — one accumulator per seq_mac, 256 instances
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 6.06654
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — a[9] = {1'b0, a_flopped[7:0]} (:4677, :4684), b[9] = {1'b0, b_flopped[7:0]} (:4678, :4685), clk = clk, reset = reset (the existing seq_mac port, driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110), out[17:0] → out_temp is replaced by out[15:0], which drives the existing saturating downcast at :4704-4716 and so out_c/matrixC*. No other input port exists in this mode; no term port is left unconnected and none needs a constant tie beyond the zero-extension bit on a and b.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110; pe_reset is the self-clearing REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR[15] bit, cfg:4868/4883) is asserted only at start-up / between matmul runs and never during an accumulation whose result is read. The baseline out_temp has no reset and no initial value, so its start value is undefined; the block's running sum is defined only after its first clear. Under this assumption the block is bit-exact with the baseline from the first post-reset edge onward.

PLAN:

What is converted. seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666-4718), instantiated once per PE in all three PE variants (processing_element:4455, processing_element_top_edge:4551, processing_element_bottom_edge:4551) — 256 instances, all on the same clk and the same effective_rst. Inside each, the fused region is exactly qmult mult_u1 (:4689, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier), the register mul_out_temp_reg (:4691-4693), qadd add_u1 (:4697, c = a + b) and the accumulator register out_temp (:4699-4701). These four are deleted and replaced by one mac_int_9x9. a_flopped/b_flopped (:4683-4686) stay in soft logic, outside the block, feeding its a/b ports.

Arithmetic is exact. DWIDTH is 8 (:74). qmult's operands are plain [7:0] vectors, so the multiply is unsigned — matching the block's unsigned ports — and zero-extension 8→9 is the allowed case in the table. The full product 255×255 = 65025 fits in 16 bits, so no product bit is lost on the way into the 18-bit sum. The baseline accumulator is 16 bits and wraps mod 2^16; the block wraps mod 2^18, and since carries propagate low→high, out[15:0] of the block equals the baseline out_temp bit for bit — the "original's narrow sum register wraps / the low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrap" row. Nothing is truncated before the sum in the baseline, so the forbidden truncate-before-sum case does not arise. The saturating downcast at :4704-4716 reinterprets out_temp[15] as a sign bit; that logic is untouched soft logic reading out[15:0], so its behaviour is unchanged.

Observability. out_temp is read by exactly two things: qadd (the accumulation itself, which goes inside the block) and the downcast at :4704-4716. The downcast is driven from the block's visible out[15:0]. mul_out_temp_reg and the qmult result are read by nothing else (qmult/qadd are leaf modules with a single consumer each). a/b are operands and are exempt; in_a/in_b continue to drive the PE's out_a/out_b shift registers unchanged. out_c → matrixC* → output_logic (:2331-2346) is unchanged.

Registers and latency. Baseline seq_mac has three register stages from a/b to the accumulator: a_flopped/b_flopped (edge k), mul_out_temp_reg (edge k+1), out_temp (edge k+2) — a product applied before edge k lands in out_temp after edge k+2. The block supplies two stages (operand register, then sum register; operands before edge k appear in out after edge k+1). The original has one stage more, so the extra stage stays outside, on the operands where it already was: a_flopped/b_flopped are kept and drive the block's ports. Then a value applied before edge k is in a_flopped at edge k, in the block's operand registers at edge k+1, and in the block's sum after edge k+2 — identical to baseline. Module latency before = 3 edges, after = 3 edges; every seq_mac output changes on the same cycle as before. The block replaces mul_out_temp_reg (by its operand register — the multiply is purely combinational between the two, so registering the operands instead of the product gives the same values on the same cycles) and out_temp (by its sum register). Neither baseline register has an enable, a load, or a reset, matching the block's contract: the block adds one product on every edge with no enable, exactly as qadd+out_temp did. The only register-control difference is that the block's registers are cleared by reset, which is the ASSUMES: line above; a_flopped/b_flopped remain unreset, as in the baseline, and during a reset window the PEs' in_a/in_b are zero anyway (PE out_a/out_b are reset at :4457-4466), so the first post-reset products agree.

Delay (rule 1). The design's slowest stage is in output_logic (reset pad → four LUTs → FF, 6.06654 ns) and the next is 6.008 ns, also output_logic; neither contains a multiply and this rewrite does not touch either, so the clock period does not improve — the gain here is area, and that is stated plainly. The stages the rewrite creates: (1) a_flopped/b_flopped → block operand ports: clock-to-Q 0.06 + one register-to-first-block wire, 1.58 ns at the median and 3.25 ns at the worst sampled wire; (2) inside the block, its fixed 2.14 ns; (3) block out → the downcast LUTs → output_logic's c_data_out* FFs: 0.06 + one block-into-soft-logic wire (0.96 median, 1.90 worst) + the same downcast and output-mux LUTs the baseline already had on that stage. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one — the block is registered, so its input wire and output wire are on different stages and no two blocks are ever in series. Soft logic that leaves a stage: the 16-bit qadd ripple adder and its routing, removed from stage (3)'s source side (it was between out_temp and itself, i.e. on the accumulator's own feedback stage, which this rewrite eliminates entirely).

Risk on stage (3). Stage (3) is the one stage whose delay could rise: its source register moves from a cluster into a DSP tile, so a cluster-output wire is traded for a block-into-soft-logic wire (0.54–1.90, median 0.96). If that path is the 6.008 ns stage, a worst-case wire could push it past 6.06654 and make it the new critical path. Working the other way, the rewrite removes roughly 8000 BLEs (see below), which shrinks the placement and should shorten output_logic's own long wires (2.64 ns into the last LUT today); I do not claim that improvement, I report the period as unchanged at 6.06654 ns.

Area. 256 blocks, all sharing one clock and one reset, pack four to a tile = 64 tiles. The 32 multiplies that remain (16 in norm, 16 in activation) are all 8×8; at the 2-per-tile density the baseline already shows for its non-quad tiles they take 16 tiles, giving 80 tiles total — 8 fewer than the baseline's 88 and well inside the device's 132. If the packer reaches 4-per-tile on those leftovers the total is 72; I do not assume it. Per PE the rewrite deletes a 16-bit adder (~16 LUTs) plus 32 flip-flops (mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp); across 256 PEs that is ~8192 BLEs, close to 800 of the ~1239 clusters the baseline uses (5.69025e7 − 88×253779 = 3.457e7, /27905 ≈ 1239). Taking a deliberately pessimistic saving of 600 clusters: area ≈ 80×253779 + 639×27905 ≈ 3.81e7, and area-delay ≈ 3.81e7 × 6.06654 ≈ 2.31e8 against the baseline 3.45201e8 — a clear improvement even before counting the tile reduction optimistically. Rule 4's caution applies: the flip-flops are the certain saving, the adder LUTs are not counted on.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. No new arithmetic, no width or signedness change, no new control signal — reset and clk are already ports of seq_mac.

Candidates left alone.

  • norm.$mul$...:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 isolated multiplies, norm:5067): each product feeds only its own element of variance_applied_data and reaches no sum at all. An isolated scalar multiply does not fit a sum-of-products/MAC mode; the block would impose a running accumulation the design does not compute. Also each is truncated to 8 bits into a register whose control is reset || ~enable_norm plus the in_data_available || norm_in_progress enable and the validity_mask[i] mux (:5047-5072) — a register with an enable and a different reset, which the block's registers cannot stand in for.
  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (sum_in=top) (the 4 whose products are later summed): the sum they feed is the pooling add in pool (:5389, four bytes added then >> 2), reached only after each product has been truncated to 8 bits and latched in variance_applied_data (:5067), published on norm.out_data through the enable_norm bypass mux (:5029) and carried across module boundaries. Three independent blockers: products truncated before the sum (explicitly disallowed — fusing and truncating after differs); each product is read by a module output port and by downstream consumers other than the sum (observability); and the pool sum is a one-shot add of four different elements with a shift, inside a register with its own reset/enable (:5364-5392), not a running accumulation that adds one product on every edge, which is the only thing mac_int_9x9 can compute.
  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 sites, activation:5479): each is data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i], truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal (:5479), and the add at :5482 happens a cycle later against data_intercept_delayed, a different value every cycle. That is not a running sum: the mode has no enable and no load and adds one product on every edge, so it cannot reproduce a one-shot product + intercept latched into a distinct register. Each product also feeds a separate output element, each is only one product in its sum, the product is truncated before the add, and both registers carry the reset reset || ~enable_activation with the in_data_available || activation_in_progress enable (:5463-5486) — a register control the block's registers do not have.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.v

response

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verification against the four revise criteria:

(a) Function change — not present. qmult (4725) is [7:0] * [7:0] unsigned, qadd (4735) is a 16-bit add, out_temp is [2*DWIDTH-1:0] = 16 bits with DWIDTH=8 (line 74). The full product 65025 fits in 16 bits, so nothing is lost into the 18-bit block sum, and out[15:0] reproduces the baseline's mod-2^16 wrap bit-for-bit. The downcast at 4704–4716 (which reinterprets bit 15 as sign) stays as soft logic reading those 16 bits, so out_c is unchanged. Latency is matched correctly: baseline has three edges (a_flopped/b_flopped 4683-4686, mul_out_temp_reg 4691-4693, out_temp 4699-4701); the block supplies two and the plan keeps the operand flops outside, giving three. Registering operands instead of the product is equivalent since the multiply between them is purely combinational.

The one real behavioral delta — reset is a declared but unused port of seq_mac, so the baseline accumulator never clears, while the block's sum register does — is unavoidable given the contract states reset is the block's only control and there is no unreset variant. The plan connects it to the signal the design already wires to that port (effective_rst, 4110, via 4455) rather than inventing one, and states the assumption explicitly. That is a sound adaptation, not a forbidden function change.

(b) New op/datatype — none. Unsigned int8 multiply and accumulate already present; 8→9 zero-extension is the admitted case; clk/reset are existing seq_mac ports.

(c) Fabricated terms — none. One product per block, mapped to the one qmult per seq_mac, feeding that instance's own accumulator. No invented term ports.

(d) Mode island — no. out[15:0] feeds the saturating downcast → out_c → matrixC* → output_logic. The result is read.

Secondary notes that do not affect the verdict: the delay claim is honest (the 6.06654 ns and 6.008 ns stages are both in output_logic with no multiply, untouched), and the stage-3 routing risk is disclosed rather than hidden. The area arithmetic is optimistic in places (the 80-tile figure assumes 2-per-tile packing for the 32 leftover multiplies, and the baseline's 88 tiles × 4 blocks exceeds the 288 multiplies the plan counts), but area estimation accuracy is not a listed revise criterion and the direction of the saving — removing 256 16-bit adders and 512 flip-flops' worth of soft logic — is sound.

Edit tpu_like.small.os.v in this directory. Local restructuring: re-express the region (e.g. a multiply array + adder tree as a fused sum-of-products) to reach the mode; preserve the region's function and role, keep the rest of the design unchanged, wire the mode's real consumers.

Goal: rewrite the RTL so the hard-block mode 'mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode' is exercised in the packed netlist,
doing real work, while preserving the design's behaviour and role.
Tier: T2

Hard-block primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
computes: acc += a*b
input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
output ports: out[18]
arithmetic: the block's operands and result are unsigned. A design operand narrower than its port must be zero-extended to the port width. A signed design operand has no exact representation on this block. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low; it is the only control of the running sum (no enable, no load), and every register is unknown until that first clear.
power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
Wire EVERY operand of the fused region onto the input ports (name them exactly as above); drive the region's real consumer from the output port. Tie unused term ports to 0. Do not leave input ports unconnected.

Region to adapt: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (module 'qmult', operation 'mac', datatype int8; products registered 1 stage(s) before their sum). The block is registered: its result is valid 2 cycle(s) after its operands, on the registers the contract describes. The module's latency must come out unchanged. Remove from the design exactly the register stages the approved plan says the block's own registers replace, add a delay only where the plan names one, and connect the block's clock and reset as the plan states.
Rules: edit Verilog source only; preserve the module's live inputs/outputs and its downstream
consumers; do not introduce operations or datatypes absent from the design unless this tier
admits a variant; keep the surrounding design unchanged for T1/T2. Do not fabricate terms merely
to match a mode's n_terms: a term the design did not compute is not a correct replacement.

Follow this approved plan:
I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: out_temp in seq_mac (the running accumulator written at tpu_like.small.os.v:4699-4701, fed by qadd add_u1 at :4697) — one accumulator per seq_mac, 256 instances
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 6.06654
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance — a[9] = {1'b0, a_flopped[7:0]} (:4677, :4684), b[9] = {1'b0, b_flopped[7:0]} (:4678, :4685), clk = clk, reset = reset (the existing seq_mac port, driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110), out[17:0] → out_temp is replaced by out[15:0], which drives the existing saturating downcast at :4704-4716 and so out_c/matrixC*. No other input port exists in this mode; no term port is left unconnected and none needs a constant tie beyond the zero-extension bit on a and b.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, systolic_pe_matrix:4110; pe_reset is the self-clearing REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR[15] bit, cfg:4868/4883) is asserted only at start-up / between matmul runs and never during an accumulation whose result is read. The baseline out_temp has no reset and no initial value, so its start value is undefined; the block's running sum is defined only after its first clear. Under this assumption the block is bit-exact with the baseline from the first post-reset edge onward.

PLAN:

What is converted. seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666-4718), instantiated once per PE in all three PE variants (processing_element:4455, processing_element_top_edge:4551, processing_element_bottom_edge:4551) — 256 instances, all on the same clk and the same effective_rst. Inside each, the fused region is exactly qmult mult_u1 (:4689, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier), the register mul_out_temp_reg (:4691-4693), qadd add_u1 (:4697, c = a + b) and the accumulator register out_temp (:4699-4701). These four are deleted and replaced by one mac_int_9x9. a_flopped/b_flopped (:4683-4686) stay in soft logic, outside the block, feeding its a/b ports.

Arithmetic is exact. DWIDTH is 8 (:74). qmult's operands are plain [7:0] vectors, so the multiply is unsigned — matching the block's unsigned ports — and zero-extension 8→9 is the allowed case in the table. The full product 255×255 = 65025 fits in 16 bits, so no product bit is lost on the way into the 18-bit sum. The baseline accumulator is 16 bits and wraps mod 2^16; the block wraps mod 2^18, and since carries propagate low→high, out[15:0] of the block equals the baseline out_temp bit for bit — the "original's narrow sum register wraps / the low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrap" row. Nothing is truncated before the sum in the baseline, so the forbidden truncate-before-sum case does not arise. The saturating downcast at :4704-4716 reinterprets out_temp[15] as a sign bit; that logic is untouched soft logic reading out[15:0], so its behaviour is unchanged.

Observability. out_temp is read by exactly two things: qadd (the accumulation itself, which goes inside the block) and the downcast at :4704-4716. The downcast is driven from the block's visible out[15:0]. mul_out_temp_reg and the qmult result are read by nothing else (qmult/qadd are leaf modules with a single consumer each). a/b are operands and are exempt; in_a/in_b continue to drive the PE's out_a/out_b shift registers unchanged. out_c → matrixC* → output_logic (:2331-2346) is unchanged.

Registers and latency. Baseline seq_mac has three register stages from a/b to the accumulator: a_flopped/b_flopped (edge k), mul_out_temp_reg (edge k+1), out_temp (edge k+2) — a product applied before edge k lands in out_temp after edge k+2. The block supplies two stages (operand register, then sum register; operands before edge k appear in out after edge k+1). The original has one stage more, so the extra stage stays outside, on the operands where it already was: a_flopped/b_flopped are kept and drive the block's ports. Then a value applied before edge k is in a_flopped at edge k, in the block's operand registers at edge k+1, and in the block's sum after edge k+2 — identical to baseline. Module latency before = 3 edges, after = 3 edges; every seq_mac output changes on the same cycle as before. The block replaces mul_out_temp_reg (by its operand register — the multiply is purely combinational between the two, so registering the operands instead of the product gives the same values on the same cycles) and out_temp (by its sum register). Neither baseline register has an enable, a load, or a reset, matching the block's contract: the block adds one product on every edge with no enable, exactly as qadd+out_temp did. The only register-control difference is that the block's registers are cleared by reset, which is the ASSUMES: line above; a_flopped/b_flopped remain unreset, as in the baseline, and during a reset window the PEs' in_a/in_b are zero anyway (PE out_a/out_b are reset at :4457-4466), so the first post-reset products agree.

Delay (rule 1). The design's slowest stage is in output_logic (reset pad → four LUTs → FF, 6.06654 ns) and the next is 6.008 ns, also output_logic; neither contains a multiply and this rewrite does not touch either, so the clock period does not improve — the gain here is area, and that is stated plainly. The stages the rewrite creates: (1) a_flopped/b_flopped → block operand ports: clock-to-Q 0.06 + one register-to-first-block wire, 1.58 ns at the median and 3.25 ns at the worst sampled wire; (2) inside the block, its fixed 2.14 ns; (3) block out → the downcast LUTs → output_logic's c_data_out* FFs: 0.06 + one block-into-soft-logic wire (0.96 median, 1.90 worst) + the same downcast and output-mux LUTs the baseline already had on that stage. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one — the block is registered, so its input wire and output wire are on different stages and no two blocks are ever in series. Soft logic that leaves a stage: the 16-bit qadd ripple adder and its routing, removed from stage (3)'s source side (it was between out_temp and itself, i.e. on the accumulator's own feedback stage, which this rewrite eliminates entirely).

Risk on stage (3). Stage (3) is the one stage whose delay could rise: its source register moves from a cluster into a DSP tile, so a cluster-output wire is traded for a block-into-soft-logic wire (0.54–1.90, median 0.96). If that path is the 6.008 ns stage, a worst-case wire could push it past 6.06654 and make it the new critical path. Working the other way, the rewrite removes roughly 8000 BLEs (see below), which shrinks the placement and should shorten output_logic's own long wires (2.64 ns into the last LUT today); I do not claim that improvement, I report the period as unchanged at 6.06654 ns.

Area. 256 blocks, all sharing one clock and one reset, pack four to a tile = 64 tiles. The 32 multiplies that remain (16 in norm, 16 in activation) are all 8×8; at the 2-per-tile density the baseline already shows for its non-quad tiles they take 16 tiles, giving 80 tiles total — 8 fewer than the baseline's 88 and well inside the device's 132. If the packer reaches 4-per-tile on those leftovers the total is 72; I do not assume it. Per PE the rewrite deletes a 16-bit adder (~16 LUTs) plus 32 flip-flops (mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp); across 256 PEs that is ~8192 BLEs, close to 800 of the ~1239 clusters the baseline uses (5.69025e7 − 88×253779 = 3.457e7, /27905 ≈ 1239). Taking a deliberately pessimistic saving of 600 clusters: area ≈ 80×253779 + 639×27905 ≈ 3.81e7, and area-delay ≈ 3.81e7 × 6.06654 ≈ 2.31e8 against the baseline 3.45201e8 — a clear improvement even before counting the tile reduction optimistically. Rule 4's caution applies: the flip-flops are the certain saving, the adder LUTs are not counted on.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. No new arithmetic, no width or signedness change, no new control signal — reset and clk are already ports of seq_mac.

Candidates left alone.

  • norm.$mul$...:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 isolated multiplies, norm:5067): each product feeds only its own element of variance_applied_data and reaches no sum at all. An isolated scalar multiply does not fit a sum-of-products/MAC mode; the block would impose a running accumulation the design does not compute. Also each is truncated to 8 bits into a register whose control is reset || ~enable_norm plus the in_data_available || norm_in_progress enable and the validity_mask[i] mux (:5047-5072) — a register with an enable and a different reset, which the block's registers cannot stand in for.
  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (sum_in=top) (the 4 whose products are later summed): the sum they feed is the pooling add in pool (:5389, four bytes added then >> 2), reached only after each product has been truncated to 8 bits and latched in variance_applied_data (:5067), published on norm.out_data through the enable_norm bypass mux (:5029) and carried across module boundaries. Three independent blockers: products truncated before the sum (explicitly disallowed — fusing and truncating after differs); each product is read by a module output port and by downstream consumers other than the sum (observability); and the pool sum is a one-shot add of four different elements with a shift, inside a register with its own reset/enable (:5364-5392), not a running accumulation that adds one product on every edge, which is the only thing mac_int_9x9 can compute.
  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 sites, activation:5479): each is data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i], truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal (:5479), and the add at :5482 happens a cycle later against data_intercept_delayed, a different value every cycle. That is not a running sum: the mode has no enable and no load and adds one product on every edge, so it cannot reproduce a one-shot product + intercept latched into a distinct register. Each product also feeds a separate output element, each is only one product in its sum, the product is truncated before the add, and both registers carry the reset reset || ~enable_activation with the in_data_available || activation_in_progress enable (:5463-5486) — a register control the block's registers do not have.

seq_mac (the 256-instance MAC, one per PE) — fused multiply + product register + accumulator into one mac_int_9x9:

  • Deleted the qmult mult_u1 instance, the mul_out_temp_reg product register, the qadd add_u1 16-bit accumulate adder, and the out_temp accumulator register, plus the now-dead mul_out/add_out/mul_out_temp wires.
  • Instantiated mac_int_9x9 mac_u1 with .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (the module's existing reset port, driven by effective_rst), .a({1'b0, a_flopped}), .b({1'b0, b_flopped}) — the design's 8-bit unsigned operands zero-extended to the 9-bit ports — and .out(mac_out).
  • out_temp is now a wire assigned mac_out[15:0], feeding the untouched saturating downcast at the end of the module, so out → out_mac → out_c → matrixC* is driven by the block's real output.
  • Latency preserved: a_flopped/b_flopped (the one extra stage the block does not supply) remain in soft logic ahead of the block; block operand register + sum register replace the two removed stages, giving the same 3-edge a/b→accumulator latency as the baseline.

Module definitions: removed qmult and qadd (no remaining instantiations) and added a blackbox declaration of mac_int_9x9 with ports clk, reset, a[9], b[9], out[18]. Updated the hierarchy comment at the top of the file.

bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.v reports "OK: synthesizes cleanly"; the census shows mac_int_9x9 256 with soft $mul down from 288 to 32 and $add from 288 to 32 (only the norm/activation multiplies and their adds remain, as planned), and $dff down from 1025 to 513.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness → after). The witness records role qmult, operation mac, int8, region qmult.$mul$...$349(sum_in=seq_mac) — i.e. the 8×8 product inside qmult whose result feeds the accumulating sum in seq_mac. After the edit, seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4665) is still the per-PE accumulating MAC of the output-stationary systolic array: processing_element instantiates it at :4454, its out drives out_c (:4452), which drives matrixC* and is latched into c_data_out_* at :2345. Same workload role (datapath MAC in a 16×16 PE array), same consumers listed in the manifest.

Real reduction, no fabricated terms (not a). The hard block's fused computation is out <= out + a*b — exactly the one product and the one accumulation that existed: the removed qmult product (mul_out_temp), its pipeline register (mul_out_temp_reg), and the removed qadd(out_temp, mul_out_temp_reg) feeding the out_temp register. Arity is 1 product + running sum, matching the witness's (single region) / sum_in=seq_mac; nothing was gathered from unrelated operands to fill an N-term dot product.

Not a mode island (not b). mac_out → out_temp (:4697) → the existing saturating downcast (:4700-4712) → out → out_c → matrix C outputs. No constants, no sink; the original consumer chain is untouched and qmult/qadd have no remaining references anywhere in the file.

Not a role break (not c). This was a datapath MAC before and is a datapath MAC after — no control-path multiply was repurposed.

Contract fit. Pipeline depth is preserved: external a_flopped/b_flopped (edge k) → hard-block operand register (k+1) → hard-block sum register (k+2), matching the original three-edge path (operand reg → mul_out_temp_reg → out_temp), with the block's own 2-cycle operand-to-out contract accounted for. Operands are zero-extended {1'b0, a_flopped} into 9 bits, which matches the original's unsigned assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier; on unsigned 8-bit ports. The 18-bit accumulator truncated to [15:0] is bit-identical to the original mod-2^16 running sum, so the downstream saturation logic sees the same value.

One behavioral delta worth recording, not disqualifying: the original out_temp register had no reset and seq_mac's reset port was dangling; the hard block's reset (its only control, per contract) is now driven by the PE's effective_rst (:4109, reset | pe_reset), so the accumulator clears with the array. That is the faithful hookup for an output-stationary PE — the alternative, tying reset to a constant, would leave the accumulator permanently unclearable — and it uses a signal already present on the block's own port list.

T2: no precision/algorithm variant introduced, so no sibling-evidence axis is required.

You are the Planner. Choose the region(s) in tpu_like.small.os.v to adapt so hard-block mode
'mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode' (Hard-block primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
computes: acc += a*b
input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
output ports: out[18]
arithmetic: the block's operands and result are unsigned. A design operand narrower than its port must be zero-extended to the port width. A signed design operand has no exact representation on this block. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low; it is the only control of the running sum (no enable, no load), and every register is unknown until that first clear.
power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
Wire EVERY operand of the fused region onto the input ports (name them exactly as above); drive the region's real consumer from the output port. Tie unused term ports to 0. Do not leave input ports unconnected.) is exercised, then write a concrete plan. Do NOT edit files —
output the header + plan only.

qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) | mac | 256 | on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 56 of the 88 tiles (the 256 element products among them; the split by family is not on file) | products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum | the only product in its sum
An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a critical path of 6.06654 ns and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132, four blocks per tile in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode. Its used logic-block area is 5.69025e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 3.45201e+08. Its slowest stage is in output_logic: no multiply, the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 1.50 ns from the pad into the first LUT, 1.91 into the third. The next slowest stage, in output_logic, is no multiply, a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT, 6.008 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Initial values. A rewrite may not rely on any register's initial value, soft or hard: the flow drops initial values, so every register starts at an unknown value and holds one until something writes it.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone. Keep every term of the original expression, including ports every instantiation ties to a constant. A term that goes on no block port (an addend beside the products) stays outside the block, added to its result; the flow flattens the design and removes an add of a constant, so such a term puts nothing on the stage and rule 2 does not count it. A bound on a port's values may be used only if the plan states it and names its source in the design, on a line of its own beginning ASSUMES:; such a rewrite is recorded as verified under that assumption. Fold a term only when it is constant inside the module itself, and say so in the plan.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A DSP tile is in one mode, and in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode one tile holds four blocks; the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock, so blocks with different reset signals cannot share a tile and each takes a tile of its own. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Measured on this architecture

Block delay, clock-to-Q and blocks per tile are from the arch file; wire and adder delays are sampled, a range with the median in parentheses.

Every integer DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile when they share one reset and one clock (the tile has one of each); blocks on different resets take a tile each.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

This mode's block holds registers

The rules above were written for blocks with no registers. This run's target mode is a registered block: its contract states a latency in cycles, where its registers sit, what its reset does and which ports are tied off. Read the rules above with the changes below.

Must hold

Register control, for this mode. The block has two register stages of its own: one on the operands, before the products, and one on the sum. A design register may go inside the block only as one of those two stages. A register on the operands, or on each product before the sum, is the first stage; the register that holds the sum is the second. The block's registers have the reset the contract states, no enable and no initial value: every one is unknown until the block's reset clears it, and the contract's timing line says when that clear lands. A design register that has an enable, a different reset behaviour, or a reset signal other than the one you connect to the block's reset port cannot be replaced by the block's register: keep it outside or leave the site alone. Connect the block's reset to the signal that reset the registers it replaces. If those registers had no reset and the block must start from a defined state (a running sum, whose value is unknown until the first clear), drive the block's reset from a reset the design asserts only at start-up, and state that assumption in the plan on an ASSUMES: line (the rewrite is then verified under it); if they had no reset and the block needs no defined start (a result that depends on the last operands alone), tie the block's reset to 0.

Latency (rule 5, and it must hold). The module's latency must be unchanged: every output carries the same value on the same cycle as before. Count the register stages the original has from the operands to the register that holds the sum, and compare with the block's latency.

the original hasthen
as many stages as the blockthe block's registers stand in place of the design's; remove exactly those and move nothing else
morethe extra stage stays outside the block, on the operands before it or on the result after it, where it was
fewerthe block adds the difference: move a register that already exists on the same path into the block's place, or delay every other value that meets the block's result by the same number of cycles so that the stage adds values from the same cycle; if neither can be done without changing when the module's outputs change, leave the site alone

State in the plan the stages the original has around each site, which of them the block replaces, and the module's latency before and after. They must be equal.

Addends and the chain input. A term the original adds to the products before the sum's register cannot be added to the block's result on the same cycle: the result is already registered. The chain input is a dedicated pin that takes only another block's chain output or a constant. If the term is a module port that every instantiation ties to a constant, connect that port to the chain input: the block is then tied off exactly where the original was, and the module still computes what it computed for every value of its ports. Any other addend needs its own register (same reset) and an add after the block, which rule 2 counts on the next stage.

Weigh

Stages (rules 2 and 3). A registered block ends the stage that reaches its operand ports and starts a new one at its result. The wire into the block and the wire out of it are on different stages. Soft arithmetic after the block is on the stage after the block's result register, not in series with the block's delay. Between its own two registers the block takes its fixed delay from the table.

Flip-flops (rule 4). The design registers the block replaces leave the soft logic.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.
