response

I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.out_temp (the per-PE running accumulator, qadd add_u1 + out_temp <= add_out)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 79
STAGE_NS: 5.27504
MAPPING: per PE instance (256 of them): a <= {1'b0, out_a[7:0]} (the PE's existing registered copy of in_a, zero-extended 8->9); b <= {1'b0, out_b[7:0]} (the PE's existing registered copy of in_b[7:0], zero-extended 8->9); clk <= clk; reset <= the PE's reset port = effective_rst (= reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110), the same net on all 256 PEs; out[15:0] -> out_temp (the 16 bits the downcast at tpu_like.small.os.v:4704-4716 reads), out[17:16] left unused. The mode has no addend/chain port in this contract, so there is no port to tie off; the accumulator's second operand is the block's own running sum, exactly as qadd added out_temp.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110) is asserted only while the array is quiescent — at power-up/global reset, or as the self-clearing pe_reset pulse written through the APB config bit PWDATA[15] (tpu_like.small.os.v:4883, auto-cleared at 4868) before a matmul is started. In that window the PE operand nets are 0: a0..a15/b0..b15 come from the systolic_data_setup registers, which are cleared by reset || ~start_mat_mul (tpu_like.small.os.v:2575, 2597, 2772, 3029, 3226), and they propagate through the array as zeros. Under this assumption (a) clearing the block's running sum at effective_rst is consistent with the baseline, whose out_temp has no reset and is undefined until something defines it, and (b) out_a/out_b[7:0] carry exactly the values a_flopped/b_flopped carry, since both are in_a/in_b[7:0] delayed one cycle and differ only in that out_a/out_b clear to 0 while effective_rst is high, when in_a/in_b are already 0.

PLAN:

What changes, against the kept first pass. The first pass already put the 256 seq_mac accumulators on mac_int_9x9 blocks and kept a_flopped/b_flopped inside seq_mac as the third register stage, "on the operands, where it was". That duplicate pair costs 16 flip-flops in every one of the 256 PEs (4096 FFs) and buys nothing: processing_element already registers the same two values one stage deep, in out_a/out_b (tpu_like.small.os.v:4457-4466, and the identical bodies in processing_element_top_edge 4553-4562 and processing_element_bottom_edge 4650-4659), for the systolic hand-off to the neighbour PE. This revision deletes a_flopped/b_flopped and sources the block's operands from those existing registers. Everything else — the block count, the reset net, the tile count, the downcast, the module boundaries — is unchanged from the measured first pass.

Register stages and latency (must be equal). Baseline seq_mac has three stages from a/b to the sum register: a_flopped/b_flopped (4683-4686), mul_out_temp_reg (4691-4693), out_temp (4699-4701). The block supplies two: its operand register and its sum register (latency 2, out valid 2 cycles after operands applied). The third stays outside, on the operand side where it was — it is the PE's out_a/out_b register, which already holds that value, so the redundant copy is removed rather than a new register added. Module latency, in_a/in_b to out_c: 3 cycles before, 3 cycles after. Timeline check: operands present in cycle 0 -> edge 1 loads out_a/out_b -> edge 2 loads the block's operand register -> edge 3 the block's sum takes that product; baseline: edge 1 a_flopped, edge 2 mul_out_temp_reg, edge 3 out_temp. Identical cycle by cycle. No register is moved across any other logic, so no other operand needs re-timing.

Register control. The two registers the block replaces (mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp) have no enable, no load, no reset and no initial value in the baseline — they match the block's registers in everything but the reset, which is covered by the ASSUMES: line above; the block's reset port is wired to seq_mac's existing (currently unconnected) reset input, i.e. effective_rst, the signal the design already uses to clear the PE. out_a/out_b, which stay outside, keep their own synchronous reset exactly as written. No register with an enable is taken inside.

Arithmetic, bit for bit. qmult is o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier on two plain [7:0] inputs, i.e. an unsigned 8x8 -> 16-bit product (the "int8" in the comments is not in this expression). Both operands zero-extend 8->9 onto the unsigned ports exactly; the product (max 65025) is exact in 18 bits. qadd is a 16-bit wrapping add into the 16-bit out_temp; the block's running sum wraps at 18 bits, and its low 16 bits reproduce that wrap exactly, which is the allowed "narrow sum register wraps" case. The downcast at 4704-4716 reads only out_temp[15:0], which is out[15:0]. No truncation happens before the sum in the baseline, so nothing is re-associated.

Observability. Inside seq_mac, a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by qmult, and mul_out_temp/mul_out_temp_reg only by qadd — nothing else in the file reads them, so deleting them hides no live value. out_temp stays visible as out[15:0] and still drives the saturating downcast that produces out, hence out_c -> matrixC* -> output_logic. out_a/out_b keep their existing consumers (the neighbour PEs); the block only takes a copy, so the systolic chain is untouched. Note one real cost: in the last column out_a and in the bottom row out_b are dangling today (the a_data_out/b_data_out assignments at 4370-4371 are commented out) and are swept away; using them as block operands brings back 8 FFs in each of those 32 PEs, +256 FFs against the -4096 removed, net -3840 FFs.

Stages touched, and the slowest stage. The design's slowest stage is in output_logic (reset pad through LUTs into a flip-flop; measured 5.27504 ns in the first pass, with 2.949 ns of that one wire). It contains no multiply and this rewrite puts nothing on it and takes nothing off it, so the rewrite cannot shorten the clock period — the gain is entirely in area, and area is not subject to the ~14.4% seed movement of the critical path. The only stage this revision alters is the operand stage: a_flopped -> block.a becomes out_a -> block.a, the same kind of path (clock-to-Q 0.06 + register-to-first-block wire 1.00-3.19, median 1.52, so ~1.58 ns typical and 3.25 ns worst), ending at the block's operand register; it does not grow. The stage in_a -> a_flopped disappears and the identical in_a -> out_a stage already exists unchanged. Longest series of blocks in any stage: one (2.14 ns between the block's own two registers), well under 5.27504. The stage after the block (out[15:0] -> OR-reduce and saturating mux, ~0.96 wire + 2 soft levels ~1.14 each ≈ 3.2 ns, then routing into output_logic) is bit-identical to the first pass and is not touched. Soft logic leaving the touched stage: none on the critical stage; what leaves the design is 256 x (16-bit adder + 16-bit product register + 16 operand FFs).

Blocks and tiles on top of the unmodified design. 256 blocks, as in the first pass — no new blocks here. All 256 share one clock and one reset (effective_rst), so they pack four to a tile: 64 tiles for the MACs plus the 15 tiles the packer keeps for the 32 unconverted soft multiplies, 79 total, as measured (baseline 88, device 132). The revision changes no tile; it removes ~3840 flip-flops of soft logic, which at the packing density implied by the first pass's own numbers (336 CLBs recovered) should take roughly 200-260 CLBs off the 903 now in use, i.e. area from 4.52425e7 toward ~3.9-4.1e7 and the area-delay product from 2.38656e8 toward ~2.05-2.16e8 at the same 5.27504 ns.

Candidates left, grouped by reason.

  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 sites, 1 instance each): left. Each is data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal[i] (tpu_like.small.os.v:5479) — an isolated scalar multiply, "the only product in its sum", feeding a per-element register; there is no common reduction, and the + data_intercept_delayed at 5482 is a separate stage a cycle later off a register, not an addend before this product's register. This mode's output is a running accumulator with no enable and no load, so it cannot deliver a bare product. Independently, the register that holds the product has an enable (in_data_available || activation_in_progress, 5474) and the reset condition reset || ~enable_activation with an else clear (5463, 5518), none of which the block's register can reproduce.
  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (flagged sop, sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 sites, "products reach no sum"): left, for the same two reasons. In the source all 16 are the same statement, variance_applied_data[i] <= mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var (tpu_like.small.os.v:5067) — one truncated product per element into its own register, no accumulator anywhere in norm; and that register carries the enable in_data_available || norm_in_progress (5056) and the reset reset || ~enable_norm with an else clear (5047, 5094), which the block's register cannot reproduce.
  • Both groups also fail the weighing test even if they fit: they sit nowhere near the slowest stage, so each converted site would be paid for in tiles and CLBs alone (~2.3 CLBs per block on a shared tile) and would raise the area-delay product.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. The rewrite adds no operation and changes no datatype; it deletes two registers and an adder per PE and re-sources two operand nets.

Risks. (1) The ASSUMES: line is the whole correctness argument for clearing the accumulator and for reusing out_a/out_b: if pe_reset were pulsed mid-matmul, or a global reset were asserted for a single cycle while data was in flight, the block would clear a sum the baseline would have kept and would lose one operand pair; both depart from the baseline only inside that window. (2) out_a/out_b gain one extra load each, a small fanout increase on an existing register-to-register path; at 0.06 ns clock-to-Q plus at most 3.19 ns of wire it stays far below the 5.27504 ns stage. (3) Bringing back the 32 edge PEs' dangling registers is a known, quantified give-back (+256 FFs) already counted above. (4) The area estimate is extrapolated from the first pass's measured CLB recovery; if the packer was already absorbing those operand flip-flops into otherwise-used clusters, the saving lands at the low end of the range — but it cannot be negative, and the critical path is untouched, so the product cannot rise.

8	// Overview
9	///////////////////////////////////
10	//This design is based on the architecture from Google's TPU v1 [1]. At its heart, 
11	//it uses a 16x16 matrix multiplication unit, instead of a 256x256 matrix multiplication 
12	//unit used by the TPU. The design uses int8 precision. This systolic matrix multiplication
13	//unit is a output stationary unit, compared to weight stationary architecture used in the TPU.
14	//The activations are stored in RAM block A, whereas the weights are stored in RAM block B. 
15	//Control and configuration are done through an APB interface, instead of a PCIe interface on 
16	//the TPU. The normalization block applies the mean and variance values to the output of the 
17	//matrix multiplication unit. Pooling unit supports 3 pooling windows - 1x1, 2x2 and 4x4. 
18	//The activation unit supports two activation functions - rectified linear unit (ReLU) and 
19	//the hyperbolic tangent (TanH). The activation unit is the last unit before the results 
20	//are written back to RAM block A, from where they can be read again into the matrix 
21	//multiplication unit for the next layer.
22	//
23	//[1] Jouppi et. al., In-Datacenter Performance Analysis of a Tensor Processing Unit, ISCA 2017
24	
25	//////////////////////////////////////
26	// Module hierarchy
27	//////////////////////////////////////
28	// top                                      (the top level design)
29	//   |--- ram                   matrix_A    (the RAM that stores matrix A (activations))
30	//   |--- ram                   matrix_B    (the RAM that stores matrix B (weights))
31	//   |--- control               u_control   (the state machine that controls the operation)
32	//   |--- cfg                   u_cfg       (unit to configure/observe registers using an APB interface)
33	//   |--- matmul_16x16_systolic u_matmul    (systolic 16x16 matrix multiplication unit)
34	//   |    |--- output_logic                 (contains logic to shift out the outputs of matmul)
35	//   |    |--- systolic_data_setup          (contains logic to shift in the inputs of the matmul)

42	//   |--- pool                  u_pool      (block that performs pooling)
43	//   |--- activation            u_activation(block that applies activation - relu or tanh)

You are an INDEPENDENT, fair Reviewer. You did not write this plan. Judge it on merit.
Target mode: mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode Tier: T2 Rule: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.
Regions the plan selects (1): qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
(op 'mac', datatype int8, consumers ['qmult']).
Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a critical path of 6.06654 ns and uses 88 DSP tiles of the device's 132, four blocks per tile in mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode. Its used logic-block area is 5.69025e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 3.45201e+08. Its slowest stage is in output_logic: no multiply, the reset input pad through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 1.50 ns from the pad into the first LUT, 1.91 into the third. The next slowest stage, in output_logic, is no multiply, a register through four LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 2.64 ns into the last LUT, 6.008 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.
The block's contract, its lines on timing, registers and tie-offs:
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
APPROVE the plan if it is correct: a legitimate adaptation does not need to be perfect, only sound.
Do NOT demand rewrites for style, minor wording, or hypothetical concerns.
REVISE ONLY for a CONCRETE, specific violation you can name: (a) it changes the computed function
when the tier forbids it; (b) it introduces an operation/datatype absent from the design and not
admitted by the tier; (c) it fabricates terms to match a mode's n_terms (products that did not
exist / do not share a reduction); (d) it wires the mode to constants or a dead sink (mode island).
A zero on an unused term port (the contract's tie-off), a zero-extension of a narrower operand,
or a constant on a port the design itself ties to that constant is not a fabricated term and
not a mode island: a block is an island only when nothing reads its result.

--- PLAN ---
I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.out_temp (the per-PE running accumulator, qadd add_u1 + out_temp <= add_out)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 79
STAGE_NS: 5.27504
MAPPING: per PE instance (256 of them): a <= {1'b0, out_a[7:0]} (the PE's existing registered copy of in_a, zero-extended 8->9); b <= {1'b0, out_b[7:0]} (the PE's existing registered copy of in_b[7:0], zero-extended 8->9); clk <= clk; reset <= the PE's reset port = effective_rst (= reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110), the same net on all 256 PEs; out[15:0] -> out_temp (the 16 bits the downcast at tpu_like.small.os.v:4704-4716 reads), out[17:16] left unused. The mode has no addend/chain port in this contract, so there is no port to tie off; the accumulator's second operand is the block's own running sum, exactly as qadd added out_temp.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110) is asserted only while the array is quiescent — at power-up/global reset, or as the self-clearing pe_reset pulse written through the APB config bit PWDATA[15] (tpu_like.small.os.v:4883, auto-cleared at 4868) before a matmul is started. In that window the PE operand nets are 0: a0..a15/b0..b15 come from the systolic_data_setup registers, which are cleared by reset || ~start_mat_mul (tpu_like.small.os.v:2575, 2597, 2772, 3029, 3226), and they propagate through the array as zeros. Under this assumption (a) clearing the block's running sum at effective_rst is consistent with the baseline, whose out_temp has no reset and is undefined until something defines it, and (b) out_a/out_b[7:0] carry exactly the values a_flopped/b_flopped carry, since both are in_a/in_b[7:0] delayed one cycle and differ only in that out_a/out_b clear to 0 while effective_rst is high, when in_a/in_b are already 0.

PLAN:

What changes, against the kept first pass. The first pass already put the 256 seq_mac accumulators on mac_int_9x9 blocks and kept a_flopped/b_flopped inside seq_mac as the third register stage, "on the operands, where it was". That duplicate pair costs 16 flip-flops in every one of the 256 PEs (4096 FFs) and buys nothing: processing_element already registers the same two values one stage deep, in out_a/out_b (tpu_like.small.os.v:4457-4466, and the identical bodies in processing_element_top_edge 4553-4562 and processing_element_bottom_edge 4650-4659), for the systolic hand-off to the neighbour PE. This revision deletes a_flopped/b_flopped and sources the block's operands from those existing registers. Everything else — the block count, the reset net, the tile count, the downcast, the module boundaries — is unchanged from the measured first pass.

Register stages and latency (must be equal). Baseline seq_mac has three stages from a/b to the sum register: a_flopped/b_flopped (4683-4686), mul_out_temp_reg (4691-4693), out_temp (4699-4701). The block supplies two: its operand register and its sum register (latency 2, out valid 2 cycles after operands applied). The third stays outside, on the operand side where it was — it is the PE's out_a/out_b register, which already holds that value, so the redundant copy is removed rather than a new register added. Module latency, in_a/in_b to out_c: 3 cycles before, 3 cycles after. Timeline check: operands present in cycle 0 -> edge 1 loads out_a/out_b -> edge 2 loads the block's operand register -> edge 3 the block's sum takes that product; baseline: edge 1 a_flopped, edge 2 mul_out_temp_reg, edge 3 out_temp. Identical cycle by cycle. No register is moved across any other logic, so no other operand needs re-timing.

Register control. The two registers the block replaces (mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp) have no enable, no load, no reset and no initial value in the baseline — they match the block's registers in everything but the reset, which is covered by the ASSUMES: line above; the block's reset port is wired to seq_mac's existing (currently unconnected) reset input, i.e. effective_rst, the signal the design already uses to clear the PE. out_a/out_b, which stay outside, keep their own synchronous reset exactly as written. No register with an enable is taken inside.

Arithmetic, bit for bit. qmult is o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier on two plain [7:0] inputs, i.e. an unsigned 8x8 -> 16-bit product (the "int8" in the comments is not in this expression). Both operands zero-extend 8->9 onto the unsigned ports exactly; the product (max 65025) is exact in 18 bits. qadd is a 16-bit wrapping add into the 16-bit out_temp; the block's running sum wraps at 18 bits, and its low 16 bits reproduce that wrap exactly, which is the allowed "narrow sum register wraps" case. The downcast at 4704-4716 reads only out_temp[15:0], which is out[15:0]. No truncation happens before the sum in the baseline, so nothing is re-associated.

Observability. Inside seq_mac, a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by qmult, and mul_out_temp/mul_out_temp_reg only by qadd — nothing else in the file reads them, so deleting them hides no live value. out_temp stays visible as out[15:0] and still drives the saturating downcast that produces out, hence out_c -> matrixC* -> output_logic. out_a/out_b keep their existing consumers (the neighbour PEs); the block only takes a copy, so the systolic chain is untouched. Note one real cost: in the last column out_a and in the bottom row out_b are dangling today (the a_data_out/b_data_out assignments at 4370-4371 are commented out) and are swept away; using them as block operands brings back 8 FFs in each of those 32 PEs, +256 FFs against the -4096 removed, net -3840 FFs.

Stages touched, and the slowest stage. The design's slowest stage is in output_logic (reset pad through LUTs into a flip-flop; measured 5.27504 ns in the first pass, with 2.949 ns of that one wire). It contains no multiply and this rewrite puts nothing on it and takes nothing off it, so the rewrite cannot shorten the clock period — the gain is entirely in area, and area is not subject to the ~14.4% seed movement of the critical path. The only stage this revision alters is the operand stage: a_flopped -> block.a becomes out_a -> block.a, the same kind of path (clock-to-Q 0.06 + register-to-first-block wire 1.00-3.19, median 1.52, so ~1.58 ns typical and 3.25 ns worst), ending at the block's operand register; it does not grow. The stage in_a -> a_flopped disappears and the identical in_a -> out_a stage already exists unchanged. Longest series of blocks in any stage: one (2.14 ns between the block's own two registers), well under 5.27504. The stage after the block (out[15:0] -> OR-reduce and saturating mux, ~0.96 wire + 2 soft levels ~1.14 each ≈ 3.2 ns, then routing into output_logic) is bit-identical to the first pass and is not touched. Soft logic leaving the touched stage: none on the critical stage; what leaves the design is 256 x (16-bit adder + 16-bit product register + 16 operand FFs).

Blocks and tiles on top of the unmodified design. 256 blocks, as in the first pass — no new blocks here. All 256 share one clock and one reset (effective_rst), so they pack four to a tile: 64 tiles for the MACs plus the 15 tiles the packer keeps for the 32 unconverted soft multiplies, 79 total, as measured (baseline 88, device 132). The revision changes no tile; it removes ~3840 flip-flops of soft logic, which at the packing density implied by the first pass's own numbers (336 CLBs recovered) should take roughly 200-260 CLBs off the 903 now in use, i.e. area from 4.52425e7 toward ~3.9-4.1e7 and the area-delay product from 2.38656e8 toward ~2.05-2.16e8 at the same 5.27504 ns.

Candidates left, grouped by reason.

  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 sites, 1 instance each): left. Each is data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal[i] (tpu_like.small.os.v:5479) — an isolated scalar multiply, "the only product in its sum", feeding a per-element register; there is no common reduction, and the + data_intercept_delayed at 5482 is a separate stage a cycle later off a register, not an addend before this product's register. This mode's output is a running accumulator with no enable and no load, so it cannot deliver a bare product. Independently, the register that holds the product has an enable (in_data_available || activation_in_progress, 5474) and the reset condition reset || ~enable_activation with an else clear (5463, 5518), none of which the block's register can reproduce.
  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (flagged sop, sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 sites, "products reach no sum"): left, for the same two reasons. In the source all 16 are the same statement, variance_applied_data[i] <= mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var (tpu_like.small.os.v:5067) — one truncated product per element into its own register, no accumulator anywhere in norm; and that register carries the enable in_data_available || norm_in_progress (5056) and the reset reset || ~enable_norm with an else clear (5047, 5094), which the block's register cannot reproduce.
  • Both groups also fail the weighing test even if they fit: they sit nowhere near the slowest stage, so each converted site would be paid for in tiles and CLBs alone (~2.3 CLBs per block on a shared tile) and would raise the area-delay product.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. The rewrite adds no operation and changes no datatype; it deletes two registers and an adder per PE and re-sources two operand nets.

Risks. (1) The ASSUMES: line is the whole correctness argument for clearing the accumulator and for reusing out_a/out_b: if pe_reset were pulsed mid-matmul, or a global reset were asserted for a single cycle while data was in flight, the block would clear a sum the baseline would have kept and would lose one operand pair; both depart from the baseline only inside that window. (2) out_a/out_b gain one extra load each, a small fanout increase on an existing register-to-register path; at 0.06 ns clock-to-Q plus at most 3.19 ns of wire it stays far below the 5.27504 ns stage. (3) Bringing back the 32 edge PEs' dangling registers is a known, quantified give-back (+256 FFs) already counted above. (4) The area estimate is extrapolated from the first pass's measured CLB recovery; if the packer was already absorbing those operand flip-flops into otherwise-used clusters, the saving lands at the low end of the range — but it cannot be negative, and the critical path is untouched, so the product cannot rise.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.v

response

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verification against the source:

  • Region and contract fit — seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4666-4718) is exactly a registered-operand → product-register → running-sum chain: a_flopped/b_flopped (4683-4686), qmult (4689 → 4725), mul_out_temp_reg (4691-4693), qadd (4697 → 4735), out_temp (4699-4701). The mode's two registers replace mul_out_temp_reg and out_temp, both of which have no enable, no load, no reset — matching the contract's "no clock enable, no path around either register, reset is its only control." Latency is preserved: in_a → out_a (edge 1) → block operand reg (edge 2) → block sum (edge 3), identical to the baseline's three edges.
  • (a) function — qmult is a plain unsigned [7:0]*[7:0] (4725), exact in 16 bits and in 18; qadd is a 16-bit wrapping add (4735) into out_temp[15:0], and the low 16 bits of an 18-bit wrapping sum reproduce it bit for bit. The downcast (4704-4716) reads only out_temp[15:0]. No re-association, no new truncation. Sourcing operands from out_a/out_b[7:0] (4457-4466, 4553-4562, 4650-4659) is the same delayed in_a/in_b[7:0], differing only while reset is high — stated as an explicit assumption, with effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (4110) confirmed as the net driving every PE's reset port (e.g. 4112, 4360).
  • (b) ops/datatypes — none added; 8→9 unsigned zero-extension is the admitted case, and the plan removes an adder and registers rather than adding anything.
  • (c) fabricated terms — 256 blocks for the 16×16 = 256 real PE accumulators, one existing product per existing running sum. No invented terms; there is no addend/chain port to fill.
  • (d) island — out[15:0] is out_temp, which still drives the saturating downcast → out_c → matrixC* → output_logic. Live consumer preserved, and out_a/out_b keep their systolic consumers (the block only taps a copy).

The left-alone candidates are also correctly described: activation 5479 and norm 5067 are isolated truncated products into registers carrying enables (in_data_available || activation_in_progress at 5474; in_data_available || norm_in_progress at 5056) and reset || ~enable_* clears (5463, 5047-ish block ending 5050-5055) — no shared reduction, so skipping them is right, not a dodge.

The disclosed give-backs (+256 FFs from reviving the 32 edge PEs' otherwise-dangling out_a/out_b, commented-out taps at 4370-4371) and the estimated-rather-than-measured area range are honestly flagged and are not violations under the stated criteria.

prompt

Edit tpu_like.small.os.v in this directory. Local restructuring: re-express the region (e.g. a multiply array + adder tree as a fused sum-of-products) to reach the mode; preserve the region's function and role, keep the rest of the design unchanged, wire the mode's real consumers.

Goal: rewrite the RTL so the hard-block mode 'mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode' is exercised in the packed netlist,
doing real work, while preserving the design's behaviour and role.
Tier: T2

Hard-block primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
computes: acc += a*b
input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
output ports: out[18]
arithmetic: the block's operands and result are unsigned. A design operand narrower than its port must be zero-extended to the port width. A signed design operand has no exact representation on this block. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
timing: out is registered inside the block on clk; it is valid 2 cycles after the input operands are applied.
registers: the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers. Operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds one product on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low; it is the only control of the running sum (no enable, no load), and every register is unknown until that first clear.
power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
Wire EVERY operand of the fused region onto the input ports (name them exactly as above); drive the region's real consumer from the output port. Tie unused term ports to 0. Do not leave input ports unconnected.

Region to adapt: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (module 'qmult', operation 'mac', datatype int8; products registered 1 stage(s) before their sum). The block is registered: its result is valid 2 cycle(s) after its operands, on the registers the contract describes. The module's latency must come out unchanged. Remove from the design exactly the register stages the approved plan says the block's own registers replace, add a delay only where the plan names one, and connect the block's clock and reset as the plan states.
Rules: edit Verilog source only; preserve the module's live inputs/outputs and its downstream
consumers; do not introduce operations or datatypes absent from the design unless this tier
admits a variant; keep the surrounding design unchanged for T1/T2. Do not fabricate terms merely
to match a mode's n_terms: a term the design did not compute is not a correct replacement.
Output rules: your edit is verified automatically and no human reads this, so do not
end with a question or an offer; state what you changed and stop.

Follow this approved plan:
I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.v:4725$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac.out_temp (the per-PE running accumulator, qadd add_u1 + out_temp <= add_out)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 79
STAGE_NS: 5.27504
MAPPING: per PE instance (256 of them): a <= {1'b0, out_a[7:0]} (the PE's existing registered copy of in_a, zero-extended 8->9); b <= {1'b0, out_b[7:0]} (the PE's existing registered copy of in_b[7:0], zero-extended 8->9); clk <= clk; reset <= the PE's reset port = effective_rst (= reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110), the same net on all 256 PEs; out[15:0] -> out_temp (the 16 bits the downcast at tpu_like.small.os.v:4704-4716 reads), out[17:16] left unused. The mode has no addend/chain port in this contract, so there is no port to tie off; the accumulator's second operand is the block's own running sum, exactly as qadd added out_temp.

ASSUMES: effective_rst (reset | pe_reset, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110) is asserted only while the array is quiescent — at power-up/global reset, or as the self-clearing pe_reset pulse written through the APB config bit PWDATA[15] (tpu_like.small.os.v:4883, auto-cleared at 4868) before a matmul is started. In that window the PE operand nets are 0: a0..a15/b0..b15 come from the systolic_data_setup registers, which are cleared by reset || ~start_mat_mul (tpu_like.small.os.v:2575, 2597, 2772, 3029, 3226), and they propagate through the array as zeros. Under this assumption (a) clearing the block's running sum at effective_rst is consistent with the baseline, whose out_temp has no reset and is undefined until something defines it, and (b) out_a/out_b[7:0] carry exactly the values a_flopped/b_flopped carry, since both are in_a/in_b[7:0] delayed one cycle and differ only in that out_a/out_b clear to 0 while effective_rst is high, when in_a/in_b are already 0.

PLAN:

What changes, against the kept first pass. The first pass already put the 256 seq_mac accumulators on mac_int_9x9 blocks and kept a_flopped/b_flopped inside seq_mac as the third register stage, "on the operands, where it was". That duplicate pair costs 16 flip-flops in every one of the 256 PEs (4096 FFs) and buys nothing: processing_element already registers the same two values one stage deep, in out_a/out_b (tpu_like.small.os.v:4457-4466, and the identical bodies in processing_element_top_edge 4553-4562 and processing_element_bottom_edge 4650-4659), for the systolic hand-off to the neighbour PE. This revision deletes a_flopped/b_flopped and sources the block's operands from those existing registers. Everything else — the block count, the reset net, the tile count, the downcast, the module boundaries — is unchanged from the measured first pass.

Register stages and latency (must be equal). Baseline seq_mac has three stages from a/b to the sum register: a_flopped/b_flopped (4683-4686), mul_out_temp_reg (4691-4693), out_temp (4699-4701). The block supplies two: its operand register and its sum register (latency 2, out valid 2 cycles after operands applied). The third stays outside, on the operand side where it was — it is the PE's out_a/out_b register, which already holds that value, so the redundant copy is removed rather than a new register added. Module latency, in_a/in_b to out_c: 3 cycles before, 3 cycles after. Timeline check: operands present in cycle 0 -> edge 1 loads out_a/out_b -> edge 2 loads the block's operand register -> edge 3 the block's sum takes that product; baseline: edge 1 a_flopped, edge 2 mul_out_temp_reg, edge 3 out_temp. Identical cycle by cycle. No register is moved across any other logic, so no other operand needs re-timing.

Register control. The two registers the block replaces (mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp) have no enable, no load, no reset and no initial value in the baseline — they match the block's registers in everything but the reset, which is covered by the ASSUMES: line above; the block's reset port is wired to seq_mac's existing (currently unconnected) reset input, i.e. effective_rst, the signal the design already uses to clear the PE. out_a/out_b, which stay outside, keep their own synchronous reset exactly as written. No register with an enable is taken inside.

Arithmetic, bit for bit. qmult is o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier on two plain [7:0] inputs, i.e. an unsigned 8x8 -> 16-bit product (the "int8" in the comments is not in this expression). Both operands zero-extend 8->9 onto the unsigned ports exactly; the product (max 65025) is exact in 18 bits. qadd is a 16-bit wrapping add into the 16-bit out_temp; the block's running sum wraps at 18 bits, and its low 16 bits reproduce that wrap exactly, which is the allowed "narrow sum register wraps" case. The downcast at 4704-4716 reads only out_temp[15:0], which is out[15:0]. No truncation happens before the sum in the baseline, so nothing is re-associated.

Observability. Inside seq_mac, a_flopped/b_flopped are read only by qmult, and mul_out_temp/mul_out_temp_reg only by qadd — nothing else in the file reads them, so deleting them hides no live value. out_temp stays visible as out[15:0] and still drives the saturating downcast that produces out, hence out_c -> matrixC* -> output_logic. out_a/out_b keep their existing consumers (the neighbour PEs); the block only takes a copy, so the systolic chain is untouched. Note one real cost: in the last column out_a and in the bottom row out_b are dangling today (the a_data_out/b_data_out assignments at 4370-4371 are commented out) and are swept away; using them as block operands brings back 8 FFs in each of those 32 PEs, +256 FFs against the -4096 removed, net -3840 FFs.

Stages touched, and the slowest stage. The design's slowest stage is in output_logic (reset pad through LUTs into a flip-flop; measured 5.27504 ns in the first pass, with 2.949 ns of that one wire). It contains no multiply and this rewrite puts nothing on it and takes nothing off it, so the rewrite cannot shorten the clock period — the gain is entirely in area, and area is not subject to the ~14.4% seed movement of the critical path. The only stage this revision alters is the operand stage: a_flopped -> block.a becomes out_a -> block.a, the same kind of path (clock-to-Q 0.06 + register-to-first-block wire 1.00-3.19, median 1.52, so ~1.58 ns typical and 3.25 ns worst), ending at the block's operand register; it does not grow. The stage in_a -> a_flopped disappears and the identical in_a -> out_a stage already exists unchanged. Longest series of blocks in any stage: one (2.14 ns between the block's own two registers), well under 5.27504. The stage after the block (out[15:0] -> OR-reduce and saturating mux, ~0.96 wire + 2 soft levels ~1.14 each ≈ 3.2 ns, then routing into output_logic) is bit-identical to the first pass and is not touched. Soft logic leaving the touched stage: none on the critical stage; what leaves the design is 256 x (16-bit adder + 16-bit product register + 16 operand FFs).

Blocks and tiles on top of the unmodified design. 256 blocks, as in the first pass — no new blocks here. All 256 share one clock and one reset (effective_rst), so they pack four to a tile: 64 tiles for the MACs plus the 15 tiles the packer keeps for the 32 unconverted soft multiplies, 79 total, as measured (baseline 88, device 132). The revision changes no tile; it removes ~3840 flip-flops of soft logic, which at the packing density implied by the first pass's own numbers (336 CLBs recovered) should take roughly 200-260 CLBs off the 903 now in use, i.e. area from 4.52425e7 toward ~3.9-4.1e7 and the area-delay product from 2.38656e8 toward ~2.05-2.16e8 at the same 5.27504 ns.

Candidates left, grouped by reason.

  • activation.$mul$...:5479$470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 (16 sites, 1 instance each): left. Each is data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal[i] (tpu_like.small.os.v:5479) — an isolated scalar multiply, "the only product in its sum", feeding a per-element register; there is no common reduction, and the + data_intercept_delayed at 5482 is a separate stage a cycle later off a register, not an addend before this product's register. This mode's output is a running accumulator with no enable and no load, so it cannot deliver a bare product. Independently, the register that holds the product has an enable (in_data_available || activation_in_progress, 5474) and the reset condition reset || ~enable_activation with an else clear (5463, 5518), none of which the block's register can reproduce.
  • norm.$mul$...:5067$368, $371, $374, $377 (flagged sop, sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5067$380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 (12 sites, "products reach no sum"): left, for the same two reasons. In the source all 16 are the same statement, variance_applied_data[i] <= mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var (tpu_like.small.os.v:5067) — one truncated product per element into its own register, no accumulator anywhere in norm; and that register carries the enable in_data_available || norm_in_progress (5056) and the reset reset || ~enable_norm with an else clear (5047, 5094), which the block's register cannot reproduce.
  • Both groups also fail the weighing test even if they fit: they sit nowhere near the slowest stage, so each converted site would be paid for in tiles and CLBs alone (~2.3 CLBs per block on a shared tile) and would raise the area-delay product.

Introduced operations/datatypes: NONE. The rewrite adds no operation and changes no datatype; it deletes two registers and an adder per PE and re-sources two operand nets.

Risks. (1) The ASSUMES: line is the whole correctness argument for clearing the accumulator and for reusing out_a/out_b: if pe_reset were pulsed mid-matmul, or a global reset were asserted for a single cycle while data was in flight, the block would clear a sum the baseline would have kept and would lose one operand pair; both depart from the baseline only inside that window. (2) out_a/out_b gain one extra load each, a small fanout increase on an existing register-to-register path; at 0.06 ns clock-to-Q plus at most 3.19 ns of wire it stays far below the 5.27504 ns stage. (3) Bringing back the 32 edge PEs' dangling registers is a known, quantified give-back (+256 FFs) already counted above. (4) The area estimate is extrapolated from the first pass's measured CLB recovery; if the packer was already absorbing those operand flip-flops into otherwise-used clusters, the saving lands at the low end of the range — but it cannot be negative, and the critical path is untouched, so the product cannot rise.

Work iteratively IN THIS SESSION: after each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.v and read its output; if it does not synthesize, fix the RTL and check again. Do NOT run place-and-route or any VTR/vpr command — that is verified separately. Stop when check.sh reports it synthesizes cleanly and its cell census shows the intended arithmetic. Then state what you changed. You are one step of an automated pipeline: your edit is verified automatically and no human reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.

response

I'll start by reading the relevant regions of the source.

Now re-source the operands in the three PE variants.

check.sh tpu_like.small.os.v reports OK: synthesizes cleanly, with the census showing mac_int_9x9 256 and the remaining soft $mul 32 (the 16 activation and 16 norm scalar multiplies, which the plan leaves untouched). The region's $mul/$add in qmult/qadd are gone from the netlist.

What I changed:

seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4672) — fused the multiply and the accumulate into one hard block:

  • Deleted a_flopped/b_flopped and their register, the qmult mult_u1 instance, mul_out_temp/mul_out_temp_reg and its register, the qadd add_u1 instance, and the out_temp register.
  • Instantiated mac_int_9x9 mac_u1 with .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (the module's existing, previously unconnected reset input, i.e. the PE's effective_rst), .a({1'b0, a}) and .b({1'b0, b}) (unsigned 8→9 zero-extension), .out(mac_out) (18 bits). No term port is left unconnected and the mode has no addend/chain port to tie off; the accumulator's second operand is the block's own running sum, as qadd added out_temp.
  • out_temp is now a wire equal to mac_out[15:0], so the saturating downcast at the end of the module — and hence out → out_c → matrixC* → output_logic — is driven unchanged from the block's output. The 16-bit wrap of the old qadd/out_temp is reproduced exactly by the low 16 bits of the block's 18-bit sum.

processing_element, processing_element_top_edge, processing_element_bottom_edge (4455, 4551/4553, 4648/4650) — the seq_mac u_mac operands are now .a(out_a), .b(out_b[DWIDTH-1:0]) instead of in_a/in_b. These are the PE's existing one-deep registered copies of the same values (used for the systolic hand-off), so they supply the third register stage that a_flopped/b_flopped used to supply, rather than adding a new register. The out_a/out_b` registers themselves, their synchronous reset, and their existing neighbour-PE consumers are untouched.

Latency in_a/in_b → out_c is unchanged at 3 cycles: edge 1 loads out_a/out_b, edge 2 loads the block's operand register, edge 3 the block's sum takes the product — matching the baseline's a_flopped, mul_out_temp_reg, out_temp edges. No other module, port, or consumer was modified; the now-unreferenced qmult/qadd module definitions remain in the file as dead code and are pruned during elaboration.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

FRAMING — refute by default. Your job is to find why this adaptation is NOT a legitimate suite
member. Assume it is contrived until the evidence shows otherwise. A rewrite that merely "makes
the mode appear in the netlist" is NOT sufficient — the mode must do real work the original
workload actually implied.

This case has already passed the deterministic structural, provenance, and (for T3) evidence
gates — do not re-litigate those mechanically. Your judgement is the legitimacy call they
cannot make: would a real designer of THIS workload ship this, or is it a mode-island / fabricated
dot-product / gratuitous variant dressed up to pass?

REJECT if any of these hold:

  • (a) FABRICATED TERMS — a fused N-term product/sum aggregates operands that did not genuinely
    share a reduction in the original; the "dot product" is manufactured to fill the mode's arity.
  • (b) MODE ISLAND / DEAD WORK — the mode's result does not flow to the design's real outputs, or
    is wired to constants / a sink that the rest of the design ignores.
  • (c) ROLE BREAK — the adapted region no longer plays the workload role the witness recorded
    (e.g. a control-path multiply repurposed as a fake datapath MAC).
  • (d) UNGROUNDED VARIANT (T3) — the precision/algorithm variant is not the one the cited sibling
    design evidences; the adaptation drifts to a workload character the suite does not contain.

ACCEPT if the adaptation is sound: the mapped arithmetic genuinely existed, the fused terms really
shared a reduction, the mode's output drives the original consumers, and the workload role is
preserved. Legitimate adaptation need not be optimal — only real.

You MUST ground your verdict: cite the specific preserved role (from the witness), the real
region(s)/reduction the mode maps onto, and — for T3 — the sibling evidence axis. A verdict you
cannot ground is a REJECT.

response

I'll examine the file before rendering a verdict.

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness). The witness records role qmult, operation mac, int8, region qmult.$mul$...$349(sum_in=seq_mac), consumer qmult. That region is the multiplier inside seq_mac whose product feeds the qadd running sum — the datapath MAC of a systolic PE in an output-stationary TPU array. After the edit, seq_mac (tpu_like.small.os.v:4672-4717) still is that PE MAC: one product per cycle accumulated into a running sum, then down-cast to DWIDTH. No control-path multiply was repurposed; same module, same operands, same role. (c) does not hold.

Real reduction, not a fabricated dot product. The mode fuses exactly the qmult multiply (i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, old line 4724) with the qadd accumulate (out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg) that the original source already chained through mul_out_temp_reg/out_temp. The accumulate is the genuine temporal reduction over the matmul's k-dimension — a 2-operand MAC, not an N-way sum assembled from unrelated operands to fill arity. (a) does not hold.

Output path is live. mac_out[15:0] → out_temp → the existing saturating down-cast → out → out_mac → out_c (4453), and out_c of every PE is matrixC<i>_<j> (e.g. pe0_0 at 4112), which is collected into c_data_out_* at 2346 — the design's real output port. Nothing is tied off or sunk. (b) does not hold.

Timing/width equivalence checks out. DWIDTH is 8 (line 74), operands are unsigned (qmult used an unsigned *), so the {1'b0, a} zero-extension to the 9×9 ports yields the identical product; taking mac_out[15:0] of the 18-bit accumulator is modularly identical to the original 16-bit wrapping out_temp. The removed a_flopped/b_flopped stage is genuinely replaced, not deleted: the hard block supplies 2 stages, and re-sourcing a/b from the PE's own registered out_a/out_b (4457, 4555, 4654 — all three PE variants register them identically at 4459-4468 etc.) restores the third. In→accumulate latency is 3 cycles before and after, and because each PE's neighbor already consumed out_a/out_b, the array-level skew is unchanged. This is the compensation a real designer would make, not a cosmetic rewrite.

Flagged deviation, not disqualifying. The original out_temp register had no reset; the hard block's contract makes reset its only control, so the accumulator now clears on effective_rst. reset was already a wired-through port of seq_mac, and clearing between matmuls is the behavior an OS systolic array actually wants, so this is a forced and benign consequence of the block contract rather than a drift in workload character. Likewise the now-uninstantiated qmult/qadd module definitions (4719-4736) are harmless dead declarations.

T2, no variant introduced, so the sibling-evidence axis (d) is not in scope.

Equivalence checks recorded on this run

Every check written into the record by verify-record, oldest first, not only the latest per candidate: a re-recording keeps the entry it replaces under verification_history. The reference is what the candidate was compared with (the run's own pristine design unless the driver named another); the boundary is the module the checks compared at; the testbench is the stimulus the simulation ran (a candidate checked with more than one testbench has an entry per testbench, the verdict one under the candidate's own label and any other under <label>@<testbench>). A formal check made in parts lists each part with its result; the entry is proven only when every part closed.

Pass 1the run's pristine designsystolic_pe_matrixnot recordedsimulation failedfail (493492 same-cycle mismatches over 1374 cycles) equiv/sim.logtimed_out (killed by the caller's cap) equiv/lec.logeffective_rst` (`reset | pe_reset`, systolic_pe_matrix:4110; `pe_reset` is the self-clearing `REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR[15]` bit, cfg:4868/4883) is asserted only at start-up / between matmul runs and never during an accumulation whose result is read. The baseline `out_temp` has no reset and no initial value, so its start value is undefined; the block's running sum is defined only after its first clear. global reset asserted only at start-up		2026-10-16 15:22:26 -0700superseded
Pass 2the run's pristine designsystolic_pe_matrixnot recordedsimulation failedfail (540078 same-cycle mismatches over 1374 cycles) equiv/revision1/sim.logtimed_out (killed by the caller's cap) equiv/revision1/lec.logeffective_rst` (`reset | pe_reset`, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110) is asserted only while the array is quiescent — at power-up/global reset, or as the self-clearing `pe_reset` pulse written through the APB config bit `PWDATA[15]` (tpu_like.small.os.v:4883, auto-cleared at 4868) before a matmul is started. In that window the PE operand nets are 0: `a0..a15`/`b0..b15` come from the `systolic_data_setup` registers, which are cleared by `reset || ~start_mat_mul` (tpu_like.small.os.v:2575, 2597, 2772, 3029, 3226), and they propagate through the array as zeros. Under this assumption (a) clearing the block's running sum at `effective_rst` is consistent with the baseline, whose `out_temp` has no reset and is undefined until something defines it, and (b) `out_a`/`out_b[7:0]` carry exactly the values `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` carry, since both are `in_a`/`in_b[7:0]` delayed one cycle and differ only in that `out_a`/`out_b` clear to 0 while `effective_rst` is high, when `in_a`/`in_b` are already 0.

Objective loop

objective
area x delay (this record predates the wirelength term, 2026-10-17: its products are area x delay)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 5.69025e+07 · critical path 6.06654 ns · wirelength 324384w · area x delay x wirelength 1.11978e+14w · area x delay 3.45201e+08 (read from entry)

The objective is area x delay: VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. Values marked w are not in the record: this record predates the wirelength term (2026-10-17), so the wirelength of each measured flow was read from the run's own vpr.out at export time (a log is read only where its area and period equal the record's for that seed), the baseline's from the baselines file, and the three-term product and W/W0 are formed from them; the record itself is unchanged. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 14.37%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 14.37%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 5 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

Pass 2 product 2.68624e+08 (-22.18%)
tier
T2
plan header
BLOCKS 256 · TILES 79 · STAGE_NS 5.27504
assumptions
effective_rst` (`reset | pe_reset`, tpu_like.small.os.v:4110) is asserted only while the array is quiescent — at power-up/global reset, or as the self-clearing `pe_reset` pulse written through the APB config bit `PWDATA[15]` (tpu_like.small.os.v:4883, auto-cleared at 4868) before a matmul is started. In that window the PE operand nets are 0: `a0..a15`/`b0..b15` come from the `systolic_data_setup` registers, which are cleared by `reset || ~start_mat_mul` (tpu_like.small.os.v:2575, 2597, 2772, 3029, 3226), and they propagate through the array as zeros. Under this assumption (a) clearing the block's running sum at `effective_rst` is consistent with the baseline, whose `out_temp` has no reset and is undefined until something defines it, and (b) `out_a`/`out_b[7:0]` carry exactly the values `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` carry, since both are `in_a`/`in_b[7:0]` delayed one cycle and differ only in that `out_a`/`out_b` clear to 0 while `effective_rst` is high, when `in_a`/`in_b` are already 0.

Your plan was built and measured: used logic area 4.52425e+07, critical path 5.27504 ns (single seed 1, no noise band applied), area-delay product 2.38656e+08, against the baseline's 3.45201e+08 (-30.86%).

This is not a failure; it is a request to do better.

What you stated in your header, against what was measured:
  BLOCKS:   256          measured 256

On this design, placement seeds move the critical path by about 14.4%; a product change inside that is not evidence of improvement.

Area is VPR's "Total used logic block area" in minimum-width transistor areas. On this architecture one DSP tile costs 253779 and one CLB 27905; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile, so a block that does not shorten the slowest stage is paid for at about 2.3 CLBs when it shares a tile and about 9 when it does not (the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock).

  norm                      1      0       0     16       0    16
  qmult                   256      0       0      1       1     0
